# Every functional requirement in Ticvai_matrix_20260621_2.xlsx, full text, one per line.
# format: <req_id> 	 <domain> 	 <sub-domain> 	 <requirement text + details, newlines as ' / '>

1.1.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell open-dated tickets for attractions.
1.1.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell dated tickets for attractions that allow access only for selected dates by guest. Special day tickets should also be supported. These are dated tickets that skip date selection by guests and only allow access on a designated day or date.
1.1.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell time-slot based tickets for attractions. The system should support: / - Creation of timeslots for a whole day or for a period  / - Configuration of capacity for each timeslot. Capacity can also be set as unlimited or defined capacity / - Ability to setup timeslots dynamically based on customer demand. / - Definition of frequency of the slots (e.g. every thirty minutes, every hour, whole day) / - Selection of precise time of entry (e.g. entry at 3.30 pm) or not (e.g. entry allowed between 2 and 4 pm)  / - Ability to setpu pre and post buffer time based on the performance start date and time. Example. Tickets are allowed to scan 30 minutes from the performance start date and time and untill 2 hours of the start time or till EOD. / - Ability to copy and paste performance for a different date
1.1.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should provide an easy-to-use interface for creation and configuration of timeslots. A calendar view should be available for the user to define the timeslot and recurrence rules. The user should be able to: / 1) Specify the frequency and days of timeslot recurrence / 2) Specify the total number of occurrences  / 3) Clone and delete occurrences
1.1.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell multiple day tickets for attractions. The number of days should be configurable. This type of ticket should require a start date to be specified before first entry.
1.1.6	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell membership passes for an attraction (e.g. annual pass, monthly pass) for configurable duration.
1.1.7	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell camp tickets. Such tickets should have configuration to allow access for X day(s) of the week for Y weeks.
1.1.8	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should be able to sell add-on items for all type of tickets. Add-ons can also be configured as a stand-alone product and able to be purchased on their own.
1.1.9	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should allow the validity period for a ticket to be configurable. For open-dated tickets, the validity period is intended to define the mandatory date by which the ticket must be used. For multiple day tickets and membership passes, the validity period is intended to define the mandatory date for the first use and/or last use.
1.1.10	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should allow the capacity for all type of ticket to be configurable. Capacity of a ticket can be configurable at multiple levels: / 1) Sales Capacity: Allow only a fixed number of tickets to be sold (i.e. only 1000 tickets for an Eid day ticket). After the sales capacity has been reached, the system should automatically reopen systems In case of any cancellation or refund. / 2) Admission Capacity: Allow tickets to be sold based on the capacity of the attraction (i.e. only 200 tickets to be sold per day for Ski Park).
1.1.11	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should allow the number of entitled access per ticket to be configurable (N entries to Y access areas). Some tickets will allow only access once per attraction while some tickets will allow unlimited access for an attraction within the allow time period. The system should provide information on the remaining access for a ticket.
1.1.12	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should allow configuration of properties for all type of tickets that define the behavior of the ticket. For example, an open-dated ticket can be available in different tiers such as normal admission, VIP admission or different age groups such as child, adult, senior.
1.1.13	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system should allow combination of multiple properties for all type of tickets. For example, there can be a VIP child ticket and a normal adult ticket.
1.1.14	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System should provide approval process to create a ticket and published onsite / online. The approval can be setup as multiple hierachy
1.1.15	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System should support creating Apple and Android tickets, which allow the customer to add tickets to Apple and Samsung Wallet.
1.1.16	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow administrators to create reusable ticket templates containing pricing, validity, capacity, access rights, add-ons, restrictions and approval workflows.
1.1.17	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support bundled ticket products combining multiple tickets, attractions, products, services, parking, F&B, retail items, memberships or vouchers into a single sellable product.
1.1.18	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support multi-attraction and multi-venue tickets with configurable usage rules, validity periods and redemption conditions.
1.1.19	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow guests to reschedule visit dates within configurable rules, fees, blackout periods and availability constraints.
1.1.20	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow exchange of tickets between ticket types, dates, timeslots and experiences while applying configurable fees, price differences and approval workflows.
1.1.21	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support ticket upgrades before or after purchase, including automatic calculation of price differences and applicable upgrade fees.
1.1.22	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support ticket downgrades according to configurable business rules, refund policies and approval requirements.
1.1.23	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support conversion of tickets into memberships, annual passes, season passes, packages or alternative ticket products.
1.1.24	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support ticket products requiring reservation prior to attendance and shall integrate with capacity management and reservation modules.
1.1.25	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow guests to join waitlists when capacity is fully booked and automatically notify guests when availability becomes available.
1.1.26	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support named tickets, anonymous tickets and partially assigned tickets with configurable ownership rules.
1.1.27	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support ticket ownership transfer between guests according to configurable policies, fees and approval workflows.
1.1.28	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support group ticket products with configurable group sizes, group pricing, quotas, approvals and group leader management.
1.1.29	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support family ticket products with configurable family composition rules, age restrictions and entitlement management.
1.1.30	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support corporate ticket allocations, corporate pricing, quotas, approval workflows and corporate account management.
1.1.31	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support recurring subscription-based ticket products with configurable billing cycles, validity periods and renewal options.
1.1.32	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support dynamic adjustment of ticket inventory and capacity based on demand forecasts, operational capacity and business rules.
1.1.33	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	AI shall recommend suitable ticket products, upgrades, bundles and promotions based on guest profile, behavior and purchase history.
1.1.34	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	AI shall recommend upgrades, add-ons and premium experiences during the purchasing journey.
1.1.35	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	AI shall automatically recommend ticket bundles, packages and complementary products to maximize guest value and revenue.
1.1.36	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support ticket lifecycle states including Draft, Pending Approval, Approved, Published, Active, Suspended, Expired and Archived.
1.1.37	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall support storage and management of tickets within the guest mobile application, Apple Wallet, Google Wallet and the TAIS digital wallet.
1.1.38	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow configuration of ticket eligibility rules including age, height, residency, nationality, membership, loyalty status, entitlement and custom business conditions.
1.1.39	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall expose APIs for ticket creation, modification, pricing, inventory, reservations, validation and integrations with third-party systems.
1.1.40	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall provide analytics and dashboards covering ticket sales, attendance, utilization, conversion rates, capacity utilization and revenue performance.
1.1.41	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	System shall allow administrators to upload documents, spreadsheets, brochures, PDFs or price lists and use AI to automatically generate ticket products, pricing structures, capacities, timeslots, validity periods, rules and add-ons.
1.1.42	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Configure unlimited ticket products and categories
1.1.43	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Configure ticket hierarchies and product bundles
1.1.44	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Support admission, membership, voucher, package and pass products
1.1.45	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Configure product attributes and metadata
1.1.46	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Support multilingual product descriptions
1.1.47	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Product versioning and audit history

1.1.128	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Functional Requirements / Create multiple donation campaigns. / Configure campaign name and description. / Define campaign validity dates. / Configure campaign images and promotional messages. / Configure campaign visibility by site, channel, or product. / Activate/deactivate campaigns without system downtime. / Support multiple concurrent campaigns.
1.1.129	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Functional Requirements / Fixed Donation / Fixed donation amount. / Multiple predefined donation values. / Variable Donation / Customer enters donation amount. / Configurable minimum and maximum donation values. / Percentage Fixed Donation / Automatically calculate donation as a fixed percentage of transaction value. / Example: 3%, 5%, 10%. / Percentage Variable Donation / Allow customer to select donation percentage during checkout. / Round-Up Donation / Round transaction value to nearest whole amount. / Automatically calculate donation difference.
1.1.130	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The solution shall support donation solicitation during the customer checkout process. /  / Functional Requirements / Donation prompt at checkout. / Optional donation acceptance. / Multiple donation campaign selection. / Donation amount review before payment. / Donation added automatically to shopping cart. / Donation displayed separately from purchased products. / Donation included in final transaction total.
1.1.131	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system shall support multiple donation campaigns within a single transaction. /  / Functional Requirements / Multiple donation campaigns in one basket. / Customer can select one or more campaigns. / Ability to allocate donations to different causes. / Campaign grouping and categorization.
1.1.132	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system shall allow donation campaigns to be associated with specific products or product groups. /  / Functional Requirements / Apply donation campaign to all products. / Apply donation campaign to selected products. / Apply donation campaign to product categories. / Apply donation campaign to ticket types. / Apply donation campaign to memberships. / Apply donation campaign to packages.
1.1.133	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The solution shall support donation collection through all POS channels. /  / Functional Requirements / Counter POS. / Self-service kiosk. / Online sales portal. / Mobile POS. / Third-party sales channels. / Corporate booking channels. / Additional Requirement /  / Donation functionality shall operate consistently across all sales channels.
1.1.134	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	The system shall separately identify donations from ticket or product revenue. /  / Functional Requirements / Donation displayed on receipt. / Separate accounting classification. / Separate taxation handling. / Donation audit trail. / Donation transaction reference. / Donation reconciliation reporting.
1.1.135	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Types	Required Reports / Operational Reports / Donations by Campaign. / Donations by Site. / Donations by Product. / Donations by Sales Channel. / Donations by Date. / Donations by User/Cashier. / Donations by Payment Method. / Financial Reports / Donation Revenue Summary. / Donation Transaction Details. / Donation Reconciliation Report. / Donation Settlement Report. / Campaign Reports / Campaign Performance. / Donation Conversion Rate. / Average Donation Value. / Round-Up Donation Analysis. / Percentage Donation Analysis. / Executive Dashboard / Total Donations Collected. / Donations by Campaign. / Monthly Donation Trends. / Campaign Comparison Dashboard.
1.2.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow management of all types of resources: / - Areas with limited capacity (e.g. Meeting rooms, Cabanas, etc.) / - Staff (e.g. Ski School Instructors, etc.) / - Objects (e.g. Strollers, Towels, etc.)
1.2.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow easy creation and tracking of resources, and link them to individual attraction experiences, so that a ticket is not only based on available time-slots but also on relevant resources.
1.2.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow management of the schedules of the staff resources with the possibility to set the parameters such as total hours, breaks, leave plan, absences. This data can be captured via manual entry or through integration with another workforce management system.
1.2.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow linking of staff resources in different combinations and time-slot capacity with individual attraction experiences such as group lessons and private lessons.
1.2.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow selection of specific instructors, staff skillset and/or specific timeslots for the staff offering experiences.
1.2.6	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should provide a calendar view for all the resources (e.g. instructors) and associated time slots (e.g. ski school session by an instructor). The calendar should support application of filters such as date range, time range, resource type and/or resource name. A resource timeslot should be selectable from the calendar view for purchase.
1.2.7	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should show the booked capacity of different time-slots to provide their availability. The capacity can be color-coded to indicate if not busy, moderately busy, or crowded within each time-slot.
1.2.8	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow check out & check in feature for rental resources. For example, towels in the water park. Inventory management of these resources should be handled in the system.
1.2.9	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow configuration of deposit requirement for rental resources. The collection and return of the deposit at the time of check-in and check-out should be handled within the system.
1.2.10	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should allow linking of a checked-out resource object to a guest profile. Specification of a rental time period for a checked-out resource object should be supported.
1.2.11	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	The system should be able to generate operational rosters for staff resources. The rosters should provide information on the staff resources associated with an attraction, their availability, booked timeslots and the tickets associated with them.
1.2.12	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System should allow to assign the resources dynamically based on the resources availability and piority. The same functionality should be available over API / Online websites
1.2.13	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Using the calendar view, system should provide an drag and drop interface to reassign the resources from one resource to another available resources. System should automatically assign the next resource in case if the assigned resource is not available
1.2.14	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Ability to define resources required for each event along with the availability schedule. The resources could be of any type:
1.2.15	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Venues & Spaces: Auditoriums, halls, rooms, stages, breakout areas.
1.2.16	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Equipment & Assets: AV systems, lighting, sound systems, projectors, chairs, booths.
1.2.17	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Staff & Personnel: Event managers, ushers, security, performers, technical crew, mascots, hosts.
1.2.18	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Resource Calendar: Centralized calendar view (daily/weekly/monthly).
1.2.19	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Availability Management: Check conflicts before assigning resources.
1.2.20	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Recurring Reservations: Block resources for repeated sessions/shows.
1.2.21	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Time Slot Management: Allocate setup, event, teardown, and maintenance times.
1.2.22	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	Multi-event Handling: Manage shared resources across parallel events.
1.2.23	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall support configurable resource types including staff, venues, equipment, rooms and rental items.
1.2.24	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall support categorization of resources.
1.2.25	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall support parent-child resource relationships.

1.2.80	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall allow employees to request shift swaps, shift transfers, shift pickups, and shift releases. Approval workflows, qualification validation, staffing rules, and manager approvals shall be configurable.
1.2.81	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall record planned shifts, actual check-in times, actual check-out times, attendance status, lateness, early departures, no-shows, overtime hours, attendance exceptions, and workforce attendance history.
1.2.82	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall support minimum staffing requirements, role coverage rules, venue staffing targets, operational staffing thresholds, workforce coverage alerts, and staffing gap detection based on demand forecasts and business requirements.
1.2.83	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall validate scheduling compliance against working-hour limits, mandatory breaks, certification requirements, overtime limits, rest periods, labor regulations, union rules (where applicable), and organizational workforce policies before assigning staff.
1.2.84	Ticketing Catalogue	Resource Management	System shall calculate labor costs, staffing budgets, scheduled labor expenses, overtime costs, event staffing costs, workforce utilization costs, and labor cost forecasts by venue, department, attraction, event, shift, and employee group.
1.3.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should allow creation of events such as performances, workshops, activities or guided-tours.
1.3.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should allow configuration of event details such as start time (date & time), end time, duration of event, capacity (amount of places that can be sold for an event), seating categories, number of seat per category, event type and seat-map view.  / System should allow for the any of those criteria to be deactivated as required.
1.3.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should support multiple occurrences/sessions, defined by a date, time, space, capacity and/or seating arrangement.
1.3.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should be able to support both general event purchase/registration or purchase/registration through email invite only.
1.3.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should have the ability to restrict a guest to single slot booking per event. This feature should be configurable as on/off for events.
1.3.6	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The systems should allow creation and configuration of spaces. It must be possible to configure different attributes for a given space such as name, capacity, usage.
1.3.7	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should allow a space to be divided in sub-spaces. Each space and sub-space can have specific rules of access.
1.3.8	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should allow sales of a seating map selection and easy, flexible space design set-up/configuration, both in full graphical display.
1.3.9	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should allow a seating plan to be created for each space. Different type of seats can be configured within the seating plan and a space can have multiple seating arrangements. The system should have the ability to configure different pricing for each type of seat.
1.3.10	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should provide the option to enable or disable seat reservation for any type of event ticket. Guests shall be able to reserve a seat from a GUI when they purchase a ticket.
1.3.11	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should offer assigned seating with multiple options for seat selection such as “pick a seat” or “best seat available.” Assigned seating should be made available both online and at the POS.
1.3.12	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should constantly update the seating arrangement as not to cause any double reservation (i.e. as not for two guests to select the same seat).
1.3.13	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	The system should have the option to allow over sales for free events.
1.3.14	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	Ability to create events with metadata (name, type, venue, date, time, organizer). The events could be free marketing, paid marketing events or show-tech events.
1.3.15	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	Define seating layouts, sections, pricing tiers, and total capacity. || Support recurring events, multiple showtimes, and blackout dates.
1.3.16	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System should provide ability to sell tickets for an event on a waitinglist in case if the capacity is not available. System should automatically allot the capacity in case of any cancellations
1.3.17	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall allow creation of reusable event templates including ticket types, pricing, capacities, seating configurations, resources and operational settings.
1.3.18	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall allow administrators to duplicate events including schedules, ticket configurations, seating plans, resources and pricing structures.
1.3.19	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support event lifecycle states including Draft, Pending Approval, Approved, Published, On Sale, Sold Out, Completed, Cancelled and Archived.
1.3.20	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support event cancellation workflows including refunds, exchanges, notifications and audit tracking.
1.3.21	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support changing event dates, times and venues while automatically updating tickets, reservations and guest communications.
1.3.22	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support assignment of venues, staff, contractors, equipment and operational resources to events.
1.3.23	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support event budgeting, cost tracking, revenue tracking and profitability analysis.
1.3.24	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support sponsor packages, sponsor entitlements, sponsorship inventory and sponsor reporting.
1.3.25	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support allocation and management of accreditation passes for staff, media, VIPs, contractors and performers.
1.3.26	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support sending email, SMS, WhatsApp and push notifications to attendees before, during and after events.
1.3.27	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall provide real-time dashboards showing attendance, check-ins, occupancy, sales, capacity utilization and operational KPIs.
1.3.28	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall provide event performance analytics including attendance, revenue, conversion rates, capacity utilization and customer engagement.
1.3.29	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support events spanning multiple venues, halls, spaces or locations under a single event.
1.3.30	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall support physical, virtual and hybrid events with configurable attendance rules and access methods.
1.3.31	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	System shall allow administrators to upload event briefs, brochures, schedules, PDFs or spreadsheets and use AI to automatically generate event configurations, sessions, ticket types, capacities, resources and schedules.
1.3.32	Ticketing Catalogue	Event Management	Ability to create and book dynamic performances based on the event start time. Dynamic performance to be chosen by customer 2 - 3 - 4 hour (for pods or Spaces)

We need the ability to change these periods easily ie , if I wanted to make Prime time 1 hr earlier on a Saturday, I need the ability to change the “Master settings” real time .

This allows for them to enter and exit the tunnel.

1.4.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	The system should allow configuration of workflows to manage the creation of new tickets and products. The workflow should involve setup of different status values (e.g. disabled, approval pending, etc.) for a ticket to provide a clear understanding of the status of a ticket.
1.4.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	The system should allow creation of product catalogue through bulk-file uploads.
1.4.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	The system should allow import and export of products in the catalogue between different environments.
1.4.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	The system should propagate any changes made to the properties of a product to the already sold tickets as well.
1.4.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support creation and maintenance of products in Draft status without exposing them to sales channels.
1.4.6	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support configurable approval workflows before products can be published, modified or retired.
1.4.7	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall allow publishing products to selected sales channels including Website, Mobile App, POS, API and Reseller channels.
1.4.8	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support future publication, activation, deactivation and automatic retirement of products based on configurable dates and times.
1.4.9	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall maintain version history of all product configurations including pricing, rules, entitlements and capacities.
1.4.10	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall allow authorized users to restore a previous version of a product configuration.
1.4.11	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support duplication of products including all associated configurations, rules, pricing and entitlements.
1.4.12	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support dependencies between products, ensuring prerequisite products are purchased when required.
1.4.13	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support grouping multiple products into a single package, bundle or offer.
1.4.14	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support archiving retired products while preserving historical sales and reporting data.
1.4.15	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall allow products to be retired from sale without impacting previously sold tickets, memberships or reservations.
1.4.16	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall identify affected tickets, reservations, memberships, events and integrations before applying product changes.
1.4.17	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall maintain a complete audit trail of product creation, modifications, approvals and publishing activities.
1.4.18	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall track product owners, creators, approvers and responsible departments.
1.4.19	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall allow products to be assigned to specific sales channels, locations, venues or customer segments.
1.4.20	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall support multilingual product descriptions, content, images and sales information.
1.4.21	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	System shall allow administrators to upload spreadsheets, brochures, PDFs or existing catalogues and automatically generate products, pricing structures, rules, entitlements and configurations using AI.
1.4.22	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	AI should significantly streamline the creation and configuration of venue seat maps by automating design, validation, categorization, and optimization tasks.
1.4.23	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Event-Specific Configurations / For multi-purpose venues, AI can: / Create different seat maps for concerts, sports events, exhibitions, and conferences. / Configure temporary seating arrangements. / Generate alternative layouts based on stage positions.
1.4.24	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Automated Seat Map Creation / Generate a complete seat map from venue drawings, CAD files, PDFs, or architectural plans. / Automatically identify seating sections, rows, and individual seats. / Convert venue layouts into a ticketing system-compatible seat map. / Detect aisles, entrances, exits, wheelchair-accessible areas, and restricted-view seats.
1.4.25	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Intelligent Seat Numbering / Automatically assign row names (A, B, C, etc.) and seat numbers based on configurable rules. / Validate numbering sequences and identify duplicates or missing seats. / Apply venue-specific numbering conventions.
1.4.26	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Seat Category Configuration / AI can recommend seat categories based on: / Distance from the stage or attraction. / Viewing angles and sightlines. / Elevation and seating tier. / Historical sales performance. / Pricing strategies.
1.4.27	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Dynamic Pricing Recommendations / Based on historical event data, AI can: / Suggest optimal pricing per seat category. / Identify high-demand areas. / Recommend premium pricing for preferred seats. / Generate revenue optimization scenarios.
1.4.28	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Visual Seat Categorization / Automatically color-code seat categories. / Generate interactive seat maps. / Highlight restricted-view or obstructed seats. / Visualize occupancy and sales patterns.
1.4.29	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Validation and Quality Assurance / AI can automatically identify: / Duplicate seat numbers. / Missing rows or seats. / Incorrect category assignments. / Accessibility compliance issues. / Capacity mismatches between the venue and ticketing system.
1.4.30	Ticketing Catalogue	Product Lifecycle	Bulk Configuration and Updates / AI can: / Apply seat categories to thousands of seats simultaneously. / Update pricing zones across multiple venues. / Clone and modify existing seat maps. / Generate configuration templates for similar venues.
1.5.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Sales Channels	The system should allow for any ticket type to be removed from sale without suspending that ticket type from use (i.e. Tickets that were already sold to guests should remain valid).
1.5.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Sales Channels	The system should stop the deletion of any ticket type for which tickets have been sold to guests already. Such ticket type can only be disabled and removed from sale.
1.5.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Sales Channels	The system should allow the user to filter the ticket by all types and available properties. For example, dated/non-dated, single-entry/multiple-entry, adult/child, standard/VIP, attraction selection, time-slot selection, etc.
1.5.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Sales Channels	The system should keep record of all tickets created. The record should include information on any changes in the status of the ticket.
1.5.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Sales Channels	The system should allow specification of rules and conditions associated with a ticket. These rules and conditions will govern the usage of the ticket and any transactions associated with a ticket such as modification or refund. The applicability of rules and conditions should be configurable according to the sales channels.
1.6.1	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support a configurable ticket resale marketplace allowing eligible ticket holders to list tickets for resale through web, mobile application, partner channels, or marketplace portals.
1.6.2	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support configurable resale eligibility rules based on ticket type, event, venue, attraction, membership level, sales channel, ticket status, purchase source, usage status, and business policies.
1.6.3	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall allow ticket holders to create, update, activate, deactivate, cancel, and manage resale listings. Listing status shall be tracked throughout its lifecycle.
1.6.4	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support configurable approval workflows including automatic approval, manual approval, and multi-level approval.
1.6.5	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall maintain real-time resale inventory and synchronize resale inventory with ticket ownership records.
1.6.6	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall allow customers to browse, search, filter, select, and purchase resale tickets through supported channels.
1.6.7	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	Upon successful resale transaction, the system shall automatically transfer ownership to the new guest while maintaining historical ownership records.
1.6.8	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall invalidate the original QR/NFC/RFID credential and generate a new credential for the new owner.
1.6.9	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall prevent duplicate usage, duplicate resale, credential sharing, ticket cloning, and unauthorized ownership changes.
1.6.10	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support configurable minimum prices, maximum prices, face value restrictions, markup limits, markdown limits, and anti-scalping policies.
1.6.11	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support configurable marketplace commissions, service fees, transaction fees, and resale fees by venue, event, product, channel, or ticket type.
1.6.12	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall validate event capacity, venue rules, reservation rules, and ticket eligibility before resale confirmation.
1.6.13	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall support cancellation of resale listings and resale transactions according to configurable policies.
1.6.14	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall send configurable notifications for listing creation, approval, rejection, sale completion, transfer completion, cancellation, and marketplace activity.
1.6.15	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall provide reporting for resale volume, resale revenue, commissions, transfer history, listing performance, venue performance, event performance, and resale trends.
1.6.16	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall maintain immutable audit logs for listings, approvals, purchases, ownership transfers, cancellations, and administrative actions.
1.6.17	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall expose marketplace functionality through APIs for websites, mobile applications, partner platforms, and third-party integrations.
1.6.18	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	System shall provide analytics dashboards displaying marketplace utilization, resale demand, pricing trends, inventory performance, and customer behavior.
1.6.19	Ticketing Catalogue	Ticket Resale Marketplace	AI shall recommend resale opportunities, pricing recommendations, demand forecasts, and inventory optimization suggestions based on marketplace activity.
2.1.1	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should have the ability to create as many sales channels as necessary by the system admin. Sales Channels creation should involve capture of all required data such as account assignment and revenue allocation.
2.1.2	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should support the configuration of products, prices, quotas, sales limits and sales schedule for each sales channels.  Some sales channels can be configured to be accessible to only pre-approved guests.
2.1.3	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should store and manage all rules for product compatibility, eligibility and pricing that will be applicable to for each sales channel. These rules will be part of the system and not external sales channels as B2C website.
2.1.4	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should ensure full integration of all internal sales channels. All associated  information (capacity, sales, etc.) must be available to multiple operators simultaneously in real-time.
2.1.5	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should have adapters for integrations with major resellers. The system provider will list all the resellers and channel managers it works with and the kind of connection (API, real-time interface, bar-codes files…).
2.1.6	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	This system should provide a Ticketing POS solution that enables the operator to sell all the tickets defined in the system including multi-day and combo tickets. The POS solution must also support entitlement redemption and wearable pick-ups.
2.1.7	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should provide POS solution that support multiple interfaces: / - Touch screen for cashiers to avoid use of a mouse and/or keyboard / - Keyboard and mouse for administrators for non-sales activity
2.1.8	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should provide POS solution that support: / - Use of a guest facing display to show cart, prices, offers, advertisement of show events and upselling promotions. The POS User Interface, flow and the information configurable by workstation.  / - Use of an RFID/NFC  reader to read or assign RFID/NFC media entitlements (RFID Ticket, wearable, NFC phone pass, etc.) / - Use of an optical reader to read barcode and QR code media / - Issue of a detailed receipt when sale is completed or email transaction receipt or by send by whatsapp / - Extensibility to integrate with the latest available technology in the market
2.1.9	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should allow the interface of POS solution to be configurable: / - Configurable hot keys on touch screen to link to a specific action. / - Configuration of various sales screens (buttons, dialogs, menus, pictures)) which are visible based on area or access rights.
2.1.10	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	This system should provide a Self-service kiosk solution that enables the guests to skip the queues at POS and purchase all type of tickets defined in the system including multi-day, combo ticket, Add Ons, Upsells. The kiosk must also support entitlement redemption and wearable pick-ups.
2.1.11	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should provide POS and Kiosk solution that allow the collection of tickets (free or not) booked on any portal. The kiosk should allow the collection of tickets using an identification number (For example: promotion code or a booking number or a mobile number. It should have the ability to do Media Swap (QR code - Wristband)
2.1.12	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should provide real-time notifications for stock replenishment of wearables/consumables (e.g. receipt roll) in the POS and self-service kiosks. Entry of new stock received should be supported.
2.1.13	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should provide the ability to capture configurable survey data. e.g. nationality, residency, guest type (individual, group, ...) in the POS and self-service kiosks. The survey should be able to trigger for every transaction / every "X no of transactions / For a specific type of Product / Specific workstation
2.1.14	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should be able to identify each ticketing kiosk individually by an ID, locate it geographically and administer it remotely. The kiosks should include a supervision interface and alert services, including the number of thermal tickets in stock, the date on which the machine was last opened, operating status (in service, intervention required, out of order).
2.1.15	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	The system should generate an alert if a machine stops working and send notification via email to IT/support team.
2.1.16	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall allow POS users to temporarily suspend an incomplete transaction and resume it later without losing selected products, guest details, discounts, payment status, or cart contents.
2.1.17	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall allow purchased tickets, receipts, vouchers, and confirmations to be delivered digitally through email, SMS, WhatsApp, guest mobile app, Apple Wallet, Google Wallet, or QR code.
2.1.18	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS and kiosk devices shall be linked to the Device Management module so administrators can monitor device status, location, software version, connectivity, errors, paper levels, and assigned workstation.
2.1.19	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS shall allow guests to earn and redeem loyalty points during checkout. Cashiers can identify guests, view loyalty balance, apply rewards, and update points after transactions.
2.1.20	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS shall validate guest memberships in real time and automatically apply membership benefits such as discounts, free entitlements, priority access, renewal offers, or upgrade eligibility.
2.1.21	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS shall support wallet top-up, wallet payment, wallet refund, balance inquiry, and wallet transaction history linked to RFID, QR, wristband, membership card, or guest profile.
2.1.22	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	AI shall recommend upgrades, add-ons, bundles, memberships, F&B products, retail items, and promotions during checkout based on guest profile and cart contents.
2.1.23	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS shall retrieve real-time prices from the Dynamic Pricing Engine based on date, timeslot, demand, capacity, promotions, customer segment, and channel.
2.1.24	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS shall identify guests through QR code, RFID/NFC, membership card, wallet, mobile app, phone number, email, or guest profile search and display relevant guest information.
2.1.25	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	Authorized staff shall be able to remotely assist guests using self-service kiosks, troubleshoot issues, and support checkout completion.
2.1.26	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall provide centralized monitoring of kiosk health including online status, stock levels, payment devices, printers, connectivity, and alerts.
2.1.27	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	POS and kiosk interfaces shall support accessibility features including large text, multilingual interfaces, high-contrast mode, and simplified navigation.
2.1.28	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	Kiosks shall provide an AI assistant to guide guests through ticket selection, promotions, FAQs, recommendations, and checkout.
2.1.29	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall provide analytics covering sales by cashier, location, device, payment method, channel, refunds, upsells, and transaction performance.
2.1.30	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	Administrators shall remotely manage POS layouts, products, promotions, payment methods, permissions, and device settings without local intervention.
2.1.31	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	Guests shall be able to start a cart on one channel and complete it on another channel while retaining all cart contents and pricing rules.
2.1.32	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall allow guests to purchase food and beverage items through self-service kiosks. The kiosk shall support menu browsing, product customization, combo meals, upsell recommendations, promotions, loyalty redemption, wallet payments, order confirmation, kitchen routing, and pickup notifications.
2.1.33	Ticketing Sales	Sales Channels	System shall allow guests to purchase retail merchandise through self-service kiosks. The kiosk shall support product browsing, inventory validation, variant selection (size, color, style), promotions, loyalty redemption, wallet payments, order confirmation, and fulfillment workflows including pickup, delivery, or collection at designated retail locations.
2.2.1	Ticketing Sales	Ticketing POS and Kiosks	The system should provide a sales interface that is as close to a website interface, considering UX design for a seamless journey.
2.2.2	Ticketing Sales	Ticketing POS and Kiosks	The system should allow creation of Mobile POS, depending on the activities, activation on demand, either on site or off site. Mobile-POS should have all the same features as a  normal POS. The sales journey on Mobile POS must be as paperless as possible, especially for payments methods and tickets. Payment can be accepted via online payment gateway or mobile payment terminal. The system provider will describe the existing features and hardware, including mobile printer.
2.3.1	Ticketing Sales	Flying POS	The system should support offline mode for POS and Kiosk: / - Ability to switch automatically to offline mode in case of server outage / network loss / - Definition of which functionality will be lost in offline mode. / - Sales of configured product types in offline mode. / - Automatic upload and reconciliation of offline transactions once online mode is restored.
2.4.1	Ticketing Sales	B2B Sales	The system should support multiple languages for the Ticketing POS, Self-Service Kiosks, websites mobile app and backend .
2.5.1	Ticketing Sales	B2B Sales	The system should expose APIs. /  / Systems allowing the guest to perform online ticket purchasing from Web-sites / mobile applications.  /  / The functionalities should include: / - Selling all the type of tickets listed in Domain 1 [Ticketing Catalogue] / - Dynamic upsell and cross-sell / - Dynamic pricing / calendar pricing  / - Ability to enter discount codes (unique / common code) / - Advance sale discount
2.5.2	Ticketing Sales	B2B Sales	The system should expose API to achieve integration for the B2C portal. It should be possible to sell all the ticket types that TIAS decide to sell on-line. The business logic will be all managed by the ticketing system without duplicating it on the frontend side.
2.5.3	Ticketing Sales	B2B Sales	The system should collect demographic data about guests and transfer to Guest 360 (CRM) through API integration. The data to be collected should be configurable.
2.6.1	Ticketing Sales	Call Center Sales	B2C website should support: / 1.Home page / 1) Banners display packages, upgrades, and discounts / 2) All available products are shown by category: Packages, Tickets, Experiences, Annual Passes, Others / 3) Prices are displayed as "From AED xxx", and promotional pricing is applied automatically /  / 2. Main purchase process / 1) Clicking a product opens its details page, showing the price calendar, session availability and pricing, usage rules, and refund/change policies; supports switching display currencies with real-time exchange rate conversion / 2) Select the number of tickets needed / 3) Click Add to Cart or proceed directly to check out / 4) On the check out page, users can log in or manually fill in the following details: name, email, mobile number, nationality, also choose to apply coupons / 5) Based on the selected product, relevant add-on options are displayed on the form page to prompt for additional purchases / 6) The following payment methods are supported: Strip Payment Gateway & NI / 7) The upgrade option is displayed as Upsell while adding products to the cart. / 8) An order confirmation email is automatically sent upon purchase, including: order number, total amount, purchase time, payment method, and a PDF e-ticket. The email also includes upgrade options and a reminder to confirm the entry method. / In the confirmation page, there should be an option to send the tickets to SMS or WhatsApp
2.6.2	Ticketing Sales	Call Center Sales	Post-order service / 1) On the order details page, users can view the order number, amount, time, payment method, user information, refund/change policies, and the QR code of the e-ticket / 2) During the valid refund/change window, users can access the entry point to request a refund or change, and complete it after paying the required fee / 3) The order details page also displays the upgrade option; users can initiate and complete the upgrade after paying the upgrade fee.

At minimum, the system should provide: / - Ability to enable/disable supervisor access requirement for a transaction / - Option to link the transaction amount to the access level required

Reissue must decrement from the ticket admission capacity if applicable. Revenue recognition must be associated with original ticket that was bought.

2.14.1	Ticketing Sales	Membership	1.General Annual card User Journey: / 1) Customers need to visit the annual card information registration page to fill in their personal information / 2) After the customer arrives at the counter, if the customer has purchased the annual card online, scan the QR code to pull the customer's annual card registration information, check the ID. / 3) If the customer has not purchased the annual card, staff should add the annual card to the cart first, then scan the membership code or manually enter the email address to pull customer's annual card registration information, and check the ID. After the review is approved and the payment is completed, staff should ask customer to choose preferred park entry option. / 4) Although the annual pass supports multiple verification methods (physical card, dynamic QR code, and Face Pass), the customer should only select one verification method. Customers may change their chosen verification method at any time before the first successful verification. However, once the initial verification is completed, the method can no longer be changed by the customer / If the customer opts for a physical card, staff will take their photo and print a card displaying the customer's full name and photograph. / 5) Customers can renew their annual pass either online or by POS, and there are exclusive discounts for renewals.

3.1.1	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	Dynamic QR Code Supports: / 1. Registration: Customers select "Digital Ticket" via the confirmation page, email, or ticket PDF to begin the enrollment process. / 2.Activation: After registration, the ticket appears as a blurred QR code in the App. It becomes clear and usable only when near park entrance beacons. / 3.Anti-fraud: Each QR code is generated dynamically using six secure parameters (OTP, timestamp, private key, ticket ID, device ID, etc.) and is not stored or visible in advance. Screenshots of the QR code are invalid and cannot be used for entry.  / 4.Offline Support: No internet connection is needed to display or validate the QR code. / 5.Device Binding: Each ticket is linked to a specific device and user to prevent unauthorized duplication or access. / 6.Ticket Transfer: Tickets can be transferred via email or app; recipients must log into the App to access and activate the QR code. / 7.Usage Restrictions: QR code only activates on the day of the visit and near park gates to ensure secure, location-based access.
3.1.2	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	Unique Code per Ticket: Generate a unique QR code for every issued ticket or pass.
3.1.3	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	Dynamic Refresh: QR codes refresh periodically (e.g., every 30–60 seconds) to prevent screenshots or duplication.
3.1.4	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	The system shall immediately invalidate dynamic QR codes when tickets are refunded, cancelled, transferred, exchanged, upgraded, or re-issued.
3.1.5	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	Access control devices shall validate dynamic QR codes using secure offline cryptographic validation without requiring continuous connectivity to the central platform.
3.1.6	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	The system shall maintain complete scan history including gate, location, timestamp, device ID, operator, validation result, and entry attempts.
3.1.7	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	Dynamic QR technology shall support memberships, annual passes, loyalty accounts, wallets, and other digital credentials in addition to standard tickets.
3.1.8	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	The system shall detect suspicious QR usage patterns including device sharing, multiple simultaneous sessions, excessive activations, and abnormal access attempts, with configurable security responses.
3.1.9	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	System shall support Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE) beacon validation, geofencing controls, and location-aware ticket activation. Dynamic tickets, memberships, annual passes, and digital credentials shall only become active when the guest is physically located within authorized venues, parks, attractions, entry gates, or designated operational zones. The mobile application shall detect BLE beacon proximity and geofence boundaries and automatically activate, deactivate, hide, or display credentials according to configured business rules. The system shall support venue-level, attraction-level, zone-level, and gate-level activation policies. Beacon identifiers, geofence definitions, activation rules, and security parameters shall be centrally configurable. Validation devices shall verify beacon context, credential permissions, ticket status, visit date, and entitlement eligibility before granting access. The solution shall support offline operation, anti-fraud protection, audit logging, and real-time monitoring of location-based activation events.
3.1.10	Admission and Access	Ticket Media	System shall support embedding entitlement information within secure QR, RFID, NFC, mobile wallet, and digital credential tokens. Embedded information may include ticket type, seat assignment, event identifier, venue access rights, timeslot, reservation details, locker assignments, membership entitlements, guest category, validity period, and other operational permissions required for offline validation. Access control devices shall validate embedded entitlements without requiring real-time connectivity to backend services.
3.2.1	Admission and Access	Access Control System	One QR to rule them all—single barcode that grants park entry, ride entitlement, meal voucher, coupon, Photo Voucher download, and re-entry rights
3.2.2	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The Ticketing PLUs shall be printed on E-tickets Ticketing PLUs shall be printed on Wristbands (paper and RFID)
3.2.3	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The Ticketing PLUs shall be printed on M-tickets
3.2.4	Admission and Access	Access Control System	It shall be possible to swap media, allowing the transferring of the attached information (swap RFID to barcode/QR code or vice versa).
3.2.5	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The ticket id can be generated in 2D barcode / The ticket id can be generated in QR code / The ticket id can be generated in RFID (ISO 15693) / The ticket ID number sequence is created by the sales system and must be randomized and always unique to limit fraud risks.
3.2.6	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The system should be able to validate tickets and membership passes with different rules based on entitlements, manage black and white list of tickets. Upon a scanning a valid ticket, the system should update that the ticket has been used.
3.2.7	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The system should support multiple validity rules access entitlements associated with a ticket. The available entry rules can be changed without required additional development effort for configuring the following entitlements: / -  Area/Attraction: attractions or area the ticket has access to (e.g. Ski Park) / -  Quantity Entry: number of allowed daily entry / -  Quantity Exit: number of allowed exit / -  Quantity Re- entry: number of same day re- entry allowed / -  Anti-pass Back: time elapse required between an exit and a re-entry / -  Required exit before re-entry / -  Required entry for exit / -  Flash: show message response or light / -  Check ID: requires the operator to verify the guest ID / -  Biometric Check: requires a biometric check / -  Biometric Check Level: specifies the scoring of the biometric comparison / -  Validity Fixed: ticket is valid from/to a specific date and time (EOY, EOM, EOW, N days after sale, N days after first usage) / -  Validity Dynamic: based on calendar (peak days, off peak, days of the week, etc.) / -  Adult Companion: whether an adult companion needs to accompany a child for an attraction / -  Allow Override: allow the ticket to be override at the gate (e.g. ticket for the wrong date) / -  Group ticket: set the number of the people allowed
3.2.8	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The system should be able to validate different media types in a transparent way,  linear barcode, QR code, RFID and NFC.
3.2.9	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The system should have the possibility to activate/deactivate the biometric check on some type of tickets. For example, membership passes, annual pass holder, multi day and multi attraction tickets.

3.2.27	Admission and Access	Access Control System	The system should have the ability to customize / configure / manage software on turnstiles and making it behave differently depending on type of card used. There should be the possibility to add external webpages on turnstile screen (if available) and enable payment technologies on Turnstile (if available). || Access control hardware supports: / 1. Real-time verification of ticket validity and real-time deduction of ticket usage / 2. The gate will display a green light and open if all of the following conditions are met: / 1) The ticket is valid. / 2) The verification method matches the one chosen by the customer / 3) The ticket is not a physical personalized ticket, unless it is the first entry for a single-day, single-park ticket. / 4) There are no segment restrictions on the ticket, or any applicable restriction has been verified (e.g., for a junior ticket with a 1.3m height limit, the hardware automatically confirms the customer's height, or auto face recognition on female limited tickets). / 5) For re-entry tickets, the customer must use the designated re-entry gate. / 3.The gate will display a yellow light, accompanied by sound and visual prompts to alert staff, in the following cases: / 1) The verification method does not match the one selected by the customer. / 2) It's a physical personalized ticket. / 3) The ticket has crowd-specific restrictions (e.g., for persons with disabilities, POD accompanying persons, the elderly, or nannies) that cannot be automatically verified.  / After verification, staff may choose to manually open the gate. / 4. Otherwise, the gate will display a red light and not open. / 5. 'Free Spin' mode can be set, the gate does not read the ticket but counts the number of rotations / 6. In an emergency, the 'Drop Arm' mode can be enabled, the gate does not count and releases / 7. If a guest wishes to re-enter the park, a UV stamp will be applied to their hand upon temporary exit. To re-enter, both the UV stamp and the ticket must be verified at the designated re-entry gate.

For example, larger turnstiles for buggies. || 1.Although the ticket supports multiple verification methods (physical card, dynamic QR code, Face Pass, Face Tag), the customer should only select one verification method. Customers may change their chosen verification method at any time before the first successful verification. However, once the initial verification is completed, the method can no longer be changed by the customer, though venue operations team may modify it if necessary. / 2.POD accompanying persons and nannies should support binding to a primary guest, and are only allowed park entry when accompanied by the bound primary ticket holder.

3.2.42	Admission and Access	Access Control System	This equipment shall be using the following technologies to read the ticket:
3.2.43	Admission and Access	Access Control System	Face Pass Enrollment || 1) Face Pass can only be registered through the App, ticket counters, or the Annual Pass counter. One facial profile may not be associated with more than one annual pass. / 2) App Registration SOP: / Customers must log in to the App, provide consent, and then complete facial registration. For minors, consent must be provided by a guardian before facial data can be captured. / 3) On-site Registration SOP: / Consent must be signed before facial data is recorded. For minors, the guardian must be present to sign the consent form and provide their email address for verification. / 4) Modify: / Customers may re-register their facial data. However, the system automatically detects the similarity between the new and previous facial data. If the difference exceeds the acceptable threshold, the update will be blocked, and the customer must contact the venue for assistance. / 5) Delete: Deletion of Face Pass can only be done through the App. If there is an active ticket that has already been verified with Face Pass, deletion is not allowed. Once deleted, any unused valid tickets should be verified with an alternative verification method. The biometric data will be permanently removed, but the consent record will be retained.
3.2.44	Admission and Access	Access Control System	Face Tag Enrollment || Unlike Face Pass, Face Tag involves the temporary storage of a customer’s facial data. Once the ticket has been fully redeemed, the biometric data is permanently deleted. Face Tag can be registered at ticketing counters or entry gates and does not require the customer to explicitly sign a consent form.

4.1.10	Bundles and Promotions	Bundles	The system should be able to allow: / - Buy X product and get X product for free.  / - Buy X product and get Y product for free. / - Buy N number of products and Get X product for free. / - Buy N number of products and get X number of products for free. / - Buy X product and Get Y percentage of the discount. / - Buy N number of products and Get X percentage of the discount. / - Buy X product and Get Y Amount of discount on another. / - Buy N number of product and Get X Amount of discount on another product. / - Buy multiple products and get cheapest item free / - Buy N number of items for a fixed price
4.1.11	Bundles and Promotions	Bundles	The system should be able to apply promotions as "buy items and get cheapest free", and further add cheaper item to the same transaction

4.3.1	Bundles and Promotions	Wallet	The system should be EMV (Euro pay, MasterCard and Visa) certified for offline and online payments.
4.3.2	Bundles and Promotions	Wallet	The system should support payment terminals (PDQ) that: / - Accept all the credit and debit cards considered / - Enter the code by Pin / - Accept swipe payment / - Accept contactless payment  / - Handle pre-authorization / - Allow refunds for card transactions / - Dynamic Currency Conversion / - Accept remote control for software updates
4.3.3	Bundles and Promotions	Wallet	The system should support payment terminals that are connected to an internal or external payment server and are not linked directly to the bank.
4.3.4	Bundles and Promotions	Wallet	The system should support payment servers that provide the following functions: / - Record transactions below the authorization threshold / - Procure authorizations over the automatic authorization threshold / - Transfer the transactions recorded in the day / - Archive the transactions for a minimum of 10 years / - Allow a degraded mode in the event of a disconnection with the network / - Reconciliation of payments status between all sales channels and bank payment gateway portal and update back in ticketing system || Customers can create a virtual wallet and top it up by cash, credit card or gift voucher. A wallet can be linked to one or multiple user accounts and can be associated with multiple wristbands. Purchases made within the park will deduct from the wallet balance. Each wristband will be checked against the linked wallet balance upon exit. If the balance is insufficient, the remaining amount can be settled before leaving the park.

Example: Use cash credit before using bonus credit.

5.3.4	F&B & Guest Management	Profile Management	The system should allow specification of a field (e.g. email) or a combination of fields (e.g. name + date of birth) to serve as the unique identifier for each guest. The system should restrict creation of guests with same unique identifier.

5.4.1	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should have the ability to integrate and exchange client information with a loyalty point system which will manage the  loyalty points credited on to the loyalty account as per the offerings of the venue. And for new guests profiles which are created - would automatically create a guest profile on the loyalty point system as well.
5.4.2	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should support loyalty point redemption in place for the guests to redeem their earned point for either products or services under venue offerings. || B2C website should support: / 1. Points acquisition: supports automatic accumulation of loyalty points based on transaction data (number of transactions or amount), and can support limiting the source of order channels / 2. Points redemption: members can use points as a payment method to deduct the total consumption amount of the order
5.4.3	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should be able to integrate with a loyalty point system which would provide the guest with a list of loyalty points according to the purchase value/purchased tickets/purchased services and add them to the guest profile.
5.4.4	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should support loyalty point system for balance enquiry operation which would capture the point balances in the guest profile and make them available to print in the receipt.
5.4.5	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should support loyalty point system  to provide access to products, promotions and prices which are meant to be for guests based on their loyalty profile tier and also customize the journey accordingly
5.4.6	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The system should provide an out-of-the-box loyalty engine module. The loyalty engine should support definition of custom levels, statuses, badges, perks, tiers, benefits, and level progression to build a unique experience for the guests. Rules can be configured to automate the loyalty steps and journey of the guest.
5.4.7	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	The loyalty program options allow to create loyalty points for a certain type of transactions and to use these points as a form of payment.
5.4.8	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Loyalty program data can be shared with a third party partner thanks to an API. For example, venue has an agreement with the airplane company Etihad allowing Etihad loyalty program members to spend their points on venue
5.4.9	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Support configurable loyalty tiers and progression rules. || System shall support configurable loyalty tiers such as Bronze, Silver, Gold, Platinum, VIP, or client-defined tiers. Tier progression shall be based on configurable rules such as spend, visits, transactions, points earned, membership status, or custom criteria.
5.4.10	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Automatically apply tier-based benefits. || System shall allow each loyalty tier to have specific benefits such as earning multipliers, discounts, priority access, free products, exclusive offers, birthday rewards, or reservation privileges.
5.4.11	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Support tier expiry and downgrade policies. || System shall support tier validity periods, tier review dates, tier renewal conditions, and automatic downgrade rules when customers do not meet the required criteria.
5.4.12	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Configure point earning rules across channels. || System shall support configurable points earning rules across ticketing, memberships, F&B, retail, events, referrals, surveys, mobile app actions, and custom business events. Rules shall support exclusions and earning multipliers.
5.4.13	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Configure redemption rules and conversion rates. || System shall support configurable points redemption rules including points-to-currency conversion, product redemption, ticket redemption, experience redemption, partial payment using points, and redemption restrictions.
5.4.14	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Support double points and promotional campaigns. || System shall support bonus point campaigns such as double points days, birthday bonuses, anniversary rewards, off-peak campaigns, event-based bonuses, and targeted campaigns for specific segments.
5.4.15	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Reward customers for successful referrals. || System shall allow guests to refer new customers and receive rewards when referral conditions are met. Rules shall define referral code, eligibility, reward type, reward timing, and fraud prevention controls.
5.4.16	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Allow pooled family loyalty points. || System shall support family loyalty accounts where points may be pooled, shared, or restricted between family members according to configurable rules controlled by the primary account holder.
5.4.17	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Support corporate loyalty programs. || System shall support loyalty programs for corporate customers, tour operators, schools, partners, and organizations, including pooled points, employee-level tracking, and organization-level reward rules.
5.4.18	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Provide digital loyalty card in app. || System shall provide a digital loyalty card within the guest mobile app and guest profile. The card shall show guest ID, tier, points balance, QR/barcode/NFC representation, and available benefits.
5.4.19	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Display balances, earning and redemption history. || System shall display available points, pending points, earned points, redeemed points, expired points, expiring points, rewards, and transaction history in the guest wallet.
5.4.20	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Redeem points for products and experiences. || System shall provide a rewards marketplace where guests can redeem points for tickets, memberships, F&B products, retail products, experiences, vouchers, partner offers, or client-defined rewards.
5.4.21	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Recommend rewards and offers. || AI shall recommend personalized rewards, campaigns, offers, and earning opportunities based on guest behavior, interests, purchase history, membership, loyalty tier, and visit patterns.
5.4.22	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Identify customers at risk of disengagement. || AI shall identify guests likely to become inactive by analyzing visit frequency, spend decline, membership expiry, reduced engagement, and historical behavior, then recommend retention actions.
5.4.23	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Recommend personalized next offers. || AI shall recommend the next best offer for each guest, such as membership upgrade, discounted event, F&B bundle, retail offer, or experience based on customer profile and behavior.
5.4.24	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Segment customers automatically. || AI shall automatically segment guests based on spend, visit frequency, preferences, purchase history, loyalty tier, membership status, family profile, and engagement level.
5.4.25	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Manage loyalty campaigns. || System shall support creation, scheduling, targeting, execution, and tracking of loyalty campaigns including points promotions, rewards campaigns, tier campaigns, birthday campaigns, and win-back campaigns.
5.4.26	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Notify users of points and rewards. || System shall notify guests about points earned, points redeemed, expiring points, tier upgrade, tier downgrade, new rewards, campaign eligibility, and personalized offers through configured channels.
5.4.27	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Provide loyalty dashboards. || System shall provide dashboards for active members, tier distribution, points issued, points redeemed, points liability, breakage, retention, campaign performance, and reward redemption.
5.4.28	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Support multiple brands and venues. || System shall support loyalty programs across multiple brands, venues, subsidiaries, or destinations with rules for shared earning, shared redemption, brand-specific benefits, and cross-brand reporting.
5.4.29	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Expose APIs for loyalty integrations. || System shall expose APIs for loyalty profile creation, points balance, earn transactions, redemption transactions, tier status, rewards, campaign eligibility, and third-party loyalty integration.
5.4.30	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Maintain loyalty transaction history. || System shall maintain a complete audit trail of loyalty transactions, manual adjustments, campaign rewards, redemptions, expirations, tier changes, and administrative actions.
5.4.31	F&B & Guest Management	Loyalty	Each tier may come with specific benefits, privileges, or incentives to encourage repeat purchases. / Tier Structure Example: / - Bronze Tier: Customers who spend more than AED 5,000 qualify for the bronze tier. / - Silver Tier: Customers who spend more than AED 10,000 qualify for the silver tier. / - Gold Tier: Customers who spend more than AED 15,000 qualify for the gold tier. / How it works: / - The system should track each customer’s total purchases. / - Once a customer’s cumulative spending crosses a tier threshold, they are automatically upgraded to that tier. / - Higher tiers may offer enhanced rewards, discounts, or exclusive privileges, incentivizing customers to increase their spending to unlock more benefits.
5.4.32	F&B & Guest Management	Unified Platform	Display memberships, wallet balances, points and vouchers in one view. || System shall provide a single guest wallet view combining membership status, loyalty points, stored value, gift cards, vouchers, benefits, credits, rewards, entitlements, reservations, and transaction history.
5.4.33	F&B & Guest Management	Unified Platform	AI provides personalized engagement and retention recommendations. || AI shall analyze membership, loyalty, wallet, ticketing, F&B, retail, reservation, and visit behavior to generate personalized offers, retention campaigns, upsell recommendations, renewal prompts, and engagement actions.

5.5.2	F&B & Guest Management	Portfolio Management	The system should allow multiple portfolios to be linked only to one account.
5.5.3	F&B & Guest Management	Portfolio Management	The system should provide the ability to move stored credit between portfolios linked to different accounts. For Example: each family member have their own portfolio with the possibility to transfer part of the stored credit between the portfolios enabled in the system. At the end, each expenditure should be tracked independently by portfolio (family member).
5.5.4	F&B & Guest Management	Portfolio Management	The system should be able to print media which are linked to guest's portfolios, this way multiple media can be availed/printed to the same guest portfolio. (examples: lost/soiled ticket reprints, website order's printing  QR code prints)
